Confirmed genuine model bug: the custom attention (line 380-388) assumes `attention_mask` is a 4D additive mask, but AutoRound passes a standard 2D `[B, S]` padding mask when batching (default batch_size=8). With batch_size=1 it accidentally broadcasts (hence the upstream "try batch_size=1" warning), but batched it fails. `use_derf=False` so only the SDPA branch matters. I'll patch it to properly convert a 2D padding mask into a combined causal+padding boolean mask.

**Root cause:** Variable-name mismatch in `phases/quantize.py`. The embedding-exclusion block filter was computed into `to_quant_block_names` (line 371), but the model_free branch defaulted a differently-named `quant_block_list = None` and lines 423–424 read that name. In the non-model_free path `quant_block_list` was never bound → `UnboundLocalError`.

Confirmed. `attention_mask` reaches the block as an **int64** `[B, S]` tensor. Since it's not a shared-cache key, auto_round stores it as a per-sample list, then `process_input_others` (data_driven.py:1099-1104) **drops all int32/int64 tensors from list-valued kwargs**, emptying the list — so `_select_inputs` crashes indexing it. `position_ids` survives only because it's a SHARED_CACHE_KEY.

Now let me record the protocol block and apply the fix.

Fix: convert `attention_mask` to a float dtype inside `SpikeWhaleModel.forward` before it's passed to the blocks, so it survives auto_round's int-filter. The attention code already does `.to(torch.bool)`, so behavior is unchanged.

The root cause is clear: `position_ids` ∈ `shared_cache_keys`, gets **emptied to `list len=0`** by the int64 filter, then something converts it to a `(8,2)` tensor. Let me trace that last conversion.

● Read special_model_handler.py
  │ ~/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/auto_round/special_model_handler.py
  └ L95:180 (85 lines read)

[auto_v3]   [agent running 810s] output: 211 lines
[auto_v3]   [agent running 840s] output: 211 lines
Root cause **confirmed**. The chain:
1. `position_ids` (int64) ∈ `shared_cache_keys` → emptied by auto_round's int-tensor filter.
2. `prepare_special_model_block_inputs` regenerates it as `arange(rotary_input.shape[1])`, but this model's block input is **4D** `[B, hc_mult=2, S, H]` (hyper-connections), so `shape[1]=2` (hc_mult) instead of seqlen → `position_ids=(8,2)`.
3. RoPE crashes: cos length 2 vs seqlen 2048.

Now the protocol block, then the fix.

● Confirm buggy regeneration shape (shell)
  │ cd /root/.cache/huggingface/modules/transformers_modules/Quazim0t0/Escarda_hyphen_86M_hyphen_B
  │ ase/9d588272b8c35e22d9d918840f168b169240293d/ && python3 - <<'PY'
  │ import torch
  │ cos_cache=torch.zeros(4096,8)
  │ # simulate the buggy regen: rotary_input = block hidden [B, hc_mult, S, H]…
  └ 6 lines…

[auto_v3]   [agent running 870s] output: 226 lines
**EVIDENCE confirmed.** Here is the mandatory protocol block:

```
COMPONENT: model_code (interacting with auto_round shared-key int-filter + 4D hidden state)
ERROR_CLASS: shape_mismatch
ROOT_CAUSE_HYPOTHESIS: auto_round drops the int64 position_ids (a shared_cache_key) during
    block replay, then regenerates it as arange(hidden.shape[1]); because this model's block
    hidden state is 4D [B, hc_mult=2, S, H] (hyper-connections), shape[1]=2 (hc_mult) not seqlen,
    producing position_ids of length 2 that mismatches the 2048 seqlen in RoPE.
EVIDENCE_CMD: instrumented repro printing rope inputs + _select_inputs position_ids
EVIDENCE_RESULT: "ROPE x=(8,10,2048,16) pos=(8,2)"; "SELECT position_ids: list len=0";
    shared_cache_keys=('position_ids','cache_position',...); regen arange(hc_mult=2)->(8,2)
VERDICT: FIXABLE
FIX_TIER: patch  (model custom code — matches existing AutoRound-workaround precedent in this file)
FIX_PLAN: Make RotaryEmbedding.forward robust: cast position_ids to long and, when its length
    doesn't match x's seq dim (x.shape[-2]), regenerate arange(seq_len) from x. This is exactly
    what auto_round intended (arange over seqlen) but on the correct axis; no-op for normal
    inference/decode where lengths already match.
SMOKE_TEST: rerun the offline autoround W4A16 RTN quantize (nsamples=128, seqlen=2048) on model.layers
```

## Summary

**Root cause:** `KeyError: 'mtp_heads.0'` — auto_round's `get_block_names` enumerates the `mtp_heads` ModuleList (a training-only Multi-Token-Prediction head, `num_nextn_predict_layers=1`) as a quantizable block group. That head is never executed during the calibration forward pass, so no inputs get cached for it, and `all_inputs.pop('mtp_heads.0')` in `_quantize_via_rtn_blockwise` crashes.

**Fix (our code, workaround tier):** Extended `compute_to_quant_block_names` in `phases/quantize.py` to also drop auxiliary train-only prediction-head block groups (`mtp`/`nextn`/`medusa`/`eagle`/`jepa`/`speculative`) in addition to the existing embedding-only filter. The resulting `to_quant_block_names` now matches only the real decoder blocks (`model.layers`). Normal models are unaffected (still returns `None`).

**Verification:**
- Smoke test: filter drops `mtp_heads` + engram tables, keeps only `model.layers`.
- Full quantize phase: all 16 layers quantized, model exported, `status: success` in 159s, CUDA still available (`True`).

**Fix (present & sound, `phases/quantize.py`):** `compute_to_quant_block_names` + `_AUX_HEAD_BLOCK_FRAGMENTS` drop auxiliary train-only prediction-head block groups (MTP/next-N/Medusa/EAGLE/JEPA) and embedding-only lookup tables, restricting `to_quant_block_names` to real decoder blocks (`model.layers`). Returns `None` for normal models (no behavior change).

## Summary

Two `float32`-buffer-vs-`bfloat16`-model dtype mismatches in the model's custom code (`model_v2.py`), caused by the quantized model running in bfloat16 while helper buffers/casts stayed float32:

1. **Line 147 (Engram hash lookup)** — `torch.matmul(compressed.float(), proj.t())`: `proj` buffer was cast to bfloat16 at load. Fixed → `proj.t().float()` so both matmul operands are float32.

2. **Line 91 (RoPE)** — `cos_cache`/`sin_cache` are float32 buffers, promoting `q`/`k` to float32 while `v` stayed bfloat16 → `F.scaled_dot_product_attention` dtype error. Fixed → cast `cos`/`sin` to `x.dtype`.

Both edits applied to the authoritative `output/runs/.../quantized_model/model_v2.py` (which transformers re-copies into the HF module cache on load). CUDA remains functional. The evaluate phase can now re-run.
